# Requirements traceability matrix

The master specification is a target, not a completion claim. IDs remain stable; append new IDs rather than renumbering. P0 = recovery/test-release gate, P1 = next platform work, P2 = later capability. `verified` is limited to the exact bounded description and tests named; `partial` means scaffolding or a narrower behavior exists; `missing` means no integrated implementation; `blocked` means a required prerequisite is unavailable; `untested` means code exists without validation. Mock results are not live or clinical validation.

Every feature bullet under master specification section 2 and every first-release acceptance criterion has a row. REC IDs track bounded recovery results separately so basic persistence does not imply a complete longitudinal patient system.

| ID | Requirement | Priority | Status | Existing implementation | Dependencies | Verification | Completion criteria | Remaining work | Milestone |

## Status interpretation

| Matrix status | Owner classification | Scope of claim |
| --- | --- | --- |
| verified | Fully implemented and tested | Only the exact bounded requirement/acceptance evidence; never a blanket clinical claim |
| untested | Implemented but unvalidated | Code exists; meaningful technical acceptance not demonstrated |
| partial | Partially implemented | Some capability exists but full requirement/validation remains incomplete |
| missing | Not implemented | No integrated implementation satisfying the requirement |
| blocked | Blocked by external dependencies | Specific absent service, data, license, credentials or external prerequisite |
| awaiting_validation | Awaiting clinical or regulatory validation | Technical implementation exists but independent clinical/regulatory acceptance is still required |

Clinical/security validation gaps are also recorded for partial capabilities; a passing synthetic test does not move them to a clinical-ready state.

## Expanded directive and historical reconciliation

All IDs above are retained. The following additional IDs preserve the newer directive and repository history, including overlaps with prior IDs. Overlap is intentional provenance, not a reason to drop a requirement. `awaiting_validation` denotes implemented behavior still requiring independent clinical/regulatory review, and must not be promoted by synthetic tests. `untested` denotes code awaiting technical validation.

# Complete-platform implementation directive reconciliation

The following requirements are additive. All earlier IDs and descriptions are preserved. Exact textual duplicates reference the existing ID rather than creating competing completion claims. New system rows describe the complete target, even when this increment implements only a bounded synthetic subset. `FLOW` and `ACCEPT` are integration/release gates; passing a local fixture does not establish live clinical service behavior.
